# Story 27.3: Production Adapter Manifest, Unit, and Deployment-Lane Qualification

Status: done

Execution gate: C0 passed on 2026-07-19 against Story 27.2's completed, reviewed handoff, was reopened on 2026-08-01 for `DW 27.3-CR42` through `DW 27.3-CR46`, and was independently re-reviewed and re-closed on 2026-08-01 after a reviewer-owned fresh clean build and 8/8 canonical execution. The first approved 2026-07-20 course correction narrows this story to C1 qualification; the later approved 2026-07-20 on-premises correction replaces `PG-AZ-1` with `PG-ONPREM-1`. C1 blocks Production lifecycle writes and Story 27.4 until the exact Production profile passes every capability, security, durability, capacity, fault, backup/restore, and physical-reclamation gate. A41 remains open and is not mutable under Story 27.3.

## Story

As a Platform Operations and security review pair,
I want the Production state-store adapter's manifests, adapter code contract, and deployment-verification lane qualified on repository-executable evidence,
So that future compliantly authored running-target C1 stories can start from a reviewed adapter without Story 27.3 claiming any C1 gate.

**Scope corrected 2026-08-01 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-08-01.** All twenty-five C1 gates are held without a registered story owner until compliant successor files with real per-gate producers are approved. Story 27.3 reaching `done` advances no C1 gate, enables no Production lifecycle write, and does not advance Story 27.4. A41 remains open and outside this story's mutation authority.

## Acceptance Criteria

6. **Given** the reviewed source SHA and the checked-in local archive producer, **when** the `production-deployment-verification` job builds the four OCI archives from that same SHA and runs the disposable-cluster verification, **then** every render, apply, disposable-context, Component-enumeration, substitution, readback, health, evidence-production, validation, and upload requirement in the existing AC6 contract must pass. The local build is current Story 27.3 execution input, not a predecessor completion from Story 30.3. Passing AC6 advances no C1 gate and enables no Production lifecycle write. Because that disposable cluster has no OpenBao runtime, the lane refuses to run against any non-disposable context, then enumerates the applied Dapr secret-store Components from the cluster and substitutes the live `spec.type` of every Component whose rendered type is `secretstores.hashicorp.vault` with `secretstores.kubernetes` before the health stages. The substituted set is discovered by type, never by a fixed set of Component names, so an added vault-typed Component cannot be silently left unpatched. Zero vault-typed Components is a passing observation, not a failure: it is the Story 31.2 end state in which the production manifests apply unmodified, and the lane must remain able to reach it. A successful enumeration that reports zero total Dapr Components is not that end state: it is a render/apply regression and fails the lane. The passing zero-vault-typed observation requires a successfully enumerated, non-empty Component set containing no vault-typed Component. A failed Component enumeration is distinguished from that end state and fails the lane. An unavoidable admission window exists between the verbatim apply and the substitution: the apply creates consumer Deployments at their manifest replica counts before the live Components can be enumerated and patched, so a consumer pod can start, attempt to load a vault-typed store, and crash-loop during that interval. The current lane cannot close that window because the live Components do not exist until after apply; the disposable-context refusal confines the risk to the disposable cluster but does not prove the Production OpenBao path. The lane records the substitution and the per-Component observed post-patch types read back from the cluster in `secret-store-substitution.json`, writes that disclosure before raising any substitution failure, produces verification evidence, validates that evidence, and uploads the evidence artifact; any failed render, verbatim apply, disposable-context assertion, Component enumeration, patch, post-patch readback, health stage, evidence production, evidence validation, or evidence upload fails the lane rather than being skipped. The resulting health proof is for a cluster that differs from the rendered Production manifests: every health-stage observation occurs after the live substitution, so no health stage exercises any vault-typed secret store and the lane does not prove the Production OpenBao/vault secret-resolution path. Added 2026-07-27 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-27 (DW 27.3-CR15); amended 2026-07-30 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-30 to disclose the deliberate runtime deviation without weakening the desired Production secret-store contract; amended 2026-07-31 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-31 (DW 27.3-CR32) to state the dynamic-enumeration contract the shipped lane implements, restore evidence production and upload to the fail list, and harmonize this copy with the Story 27.3 copy; amended 2026-08-01 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-08-01 to distinguish zero total Components from the non-empty zero-vault-typed Story 31.2 end state; amended 2026-08-01 by approved Sprint Change Proposal `sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-01-story-27-3-review-readiness-blockers.md` to disclose the unclosable post-apply/pre-substitution admission window.
7. **Given** the `Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry.Tests` unit lane and a fresh Release build of it from the reviewed source,
   **When** the lane executes under the **Checkpoint Execution Contract** below,
   **Then** it proves the `state.postgresql/v2` access-telemetry adapter contract — transactional record-plus-expiry-index write and delete, ETag and `FirstWrite` semantics, `Conflict`/`StaleIndex`/`VerificationFailed`/`AlreadyAbsent` classification, ordering parity, and bucket-identity matching — against the in-process fakes. *Added 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28, ratifying checkpoint C3. This criterion proves the adapter code contract, never the running target; it advances no C1 gate, enables no Production lifecycle write, and does not satisfy C1.11, whose cross-tenant denial must be observed against the running profile.*

8. **Given** the `ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests` lane and a fresh Release build of `Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests` from the reviewed source,
   **When** the lane executes under its own class selector under the **Checkpoint Execution Contract** below,
   **Then** it statically binds the reviewed `PG-ONPREM-1` manifests — `connectionString` resolving only through `secretKeyRef`, `actorStateStore: "true"`, `skipVerify`/`tlsServerName`, the ordered first-match `pg_hba` rules, least-privilege init-SQL grants, the RBAC secret-reader Roles, the deny-default lifecycle ACL, and the connection-pool arithmetic against `max_connections` including `maxSurge`/`maxUnavailable`. *Added 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28, ratifying checkpoint C4. This criterion proves the manifests say what they must, never that the running deployment behaves as they say; it advances no C1 gate and enables no Production lifecycle write.*

### Historical C1 Scope Transfer Record — Story 27.5 (Non-Binding; 2026-07-28)

The approved 2026-07-28 course correction executes the Administrator decision of 2026-07-27 (eighth-invocation code review): the blocked C1 gate set moves to a newly numbered story.

**To Story 27.5 (Epic 27)** — the thirteen C1 gates that carry no executable producer while the `PG-ONPREM-1` lifecycle environment is disabled: **C1.1** CRUD, **C1.2** strong reads, **C1.3** ETags, **C1.4** rollback-atomic multi-key transactions, **C1.5** TTL, **C1.6** actor reactivation, **C1.7** Placement/Scheduler/reminder recovery, **C1.8** request bounds, **C1.9** two-writer 500 events/s throughput, **C1.10** 150,000-record purge catch-up, **C1.11** isolation, **C1.12** encryption, and **C1.14** cohort-attributable physical reclamation. ### Historical Dependency Transfer Record — Stories 30.1 and 31.1 (Non-Binding; 2026-07-26)

The approved 2026-07-26 course correction executes the two splits the Administrator approved on 2026-07-21 during code review.

**To Story 30.1 (Epic 30, DW 27.3-CR5)** — the four-image container release and partial-recovery pipeline, with its CI wiring, tooling, tests, and the release-runbook four-image expansion.

**To Story 31.1 (Epic 31, DW 27.3-CR6)** — the OpenBao `hexalith-keys` platform and the runtime Dapr `secretstore` migration from Kubernetes Secrets to `hashicorp.vault` for the `eventstore` and `memories` scopes. Story 27.3 retains the access-telemetry-specific secret components and the `PG-ONPREM-1` secret backing, which remain in adapter scope.

**Environment prerequisite, not a story dependency:** the `PG-ONPREM-1` connection string, including `sslmode=verify-full`, resolves from the deployed OpenBao platform. That platform is already running — the 2026-07-20 live probe loaded the store — so Story 31.1 formalizes ownership and hardening of an operational platform rather than standing one up. Story 27.3's C1 probe is not blocked on Story 31.1. **Corrected 2026-07-26 by code review (chunk 3b), per the Administrator decision recorded below:** a forward dependency on Story 30.1 *does* exist and is accepted. The 27.3-retained `tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1` declares `-AccessTelemetryArchive` and `-AccessTelemetryClockArchive` as `[Parameter(Mandatory)]`, and both `.tar.gz` archives are produced only by the transferred `tools/publish-containers.ps1` (Story 30.1); the C1 reopen trigger recorded in the Debug Log additionally requires signed registry publication for all four images, which is Story 30.1 acceptance scope. Story 30.1 must therefore not regress this verification lane. The earlier blanket claim that "no forward dependency is created" was wrong and is superseded by this paragraph.

Neither transferred block is executable under Story 27.3, and neither may be claimed as Story 27.3 evidence. Both target stories are `backlog`.

**Owner update 2026-07-27 (approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-27, DW 27.3-CR16).** The two paragraphs above are the append-only record of the 2026-07-26 transfer and are not rewritten, but their target stories have since been split and their owners have moved. The Story 30.1 block is now Stories 30.1, 30.3, 30.4 and 30.5; in particular `tools/publish-containers.ps1` - the producer of the two `.tar.gz` archives this story's `tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1` declares `[Parameter(Mandatory)]` - is now **Story 30.3**-owned, so the accepted forward dependency recorded above is on Story 30.3, not Story 30.1. `tests/Hexalith.Memories.Cli.Tests/Ci/CiTestInventoryTests.cs` remains Story 30.1-owned. The Story 31.1 block is now Stories 31.1 and 31.2. All six target stories remain `backlog`.

**Dependency correction 2026-08-03.** The preceding 2026-07-26/27 dependency decisions remain byte-stable append-only history and are superseded for current execution. The checked-in `tools/publish-containers.ps1` producer runs before `tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1` in Story 27.3's own `production-deployment-verification` job, producing all four local archives from the same reviewed source SHA. Story 30.3 is therefore not a predecessor-status gate; its independent backlog obligation is future registry-publication hardening and non-regression of the four stable archive names and their Story 27.3 lane compatibility.

Seven new cases in `InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStoreContractTests` pin each behaviour.
- [x] [Review][Patch] The deployment-guard helpers are structurally unsound, so several guards assert less than they claim - `ReadComponentMetadata` takes the next `value:` anywhere after the name with no item bound, returning a neighbour's value for any `secretKeyRef`-backed key; `ReadYamlSequence` runs to end-of-document when the terminator is absent and inspects only the first key occurrence; `max_connections` and `reserved_connections` are searched only in the StatefulSet document and only at the first occurrence, so setting either through `access-telemetry-postgresql-config` voids the check; the pg_hba assertions are presence-only and ignore first-match-wins ordering, so a bare `host ... scram-sha-256` line inserted before the reject rules accepts plaintext TCP while all four assertions pass; and the ACL guards are case-sensitive substring counts, so `httpVerb: ["delete"]` bypasses `ShouldNotContain("DELETE")`. [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests/Deployment/ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs:442] — applied 2026-07-26, all five: `ReadComponentMetadata` binds to its own `- name:` item and requires exactly one inline value (with a separate `ReadComponentMetadataSecretRef` for `secretKeyRef`-backed entries); `ReadYamlSequence` binds by indentation, requires the key to occur exactly once, and handles a key that is itself a sequence item; `max_connections`/`reserved_connections` are searched across the whole rendered manifest and must occur exactly once; the pg_hba rules are compared as an exact ordered list so a `host` rule inserted above the reject rules fails; and the ACL verb exclusions are case-insensitive. Mutation-proven: a plaintext-capable `host` rule above the rejects, a lowercase `delete` verb, and a repointed `connectionString` secret each fail the class.
- [x] [Review][Patch] The connection-pool guard hardcodes `c1WriterReplicas = 2` while the rendered overlay is `replicas: 0` with no `strategy:` block, so the constant is bound to nothing - a scale to three replicas or a default RollingUpdate surge gives `3 x 40 + 13 = 133` against `max_connections=100` and the test still passes. Read `spec.replicas` and add the surge ceiling. [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests/Deployment/ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs:326] — applied 2026-07-26: the guard reads `spec.replicas` and `maxSurge` out of the rendered overlay and computes peak concurrent pods as `max(declared, C1 probe) + maxSurge`. Because the Kubernetes default surge is 25% and would round up to a third sidecar pool (3 x 40 + 13 = 133 > 100), the lifecycle Deployment now pins `strategy.rollingUpdate.maxSurge: 0` with `maxUnavailable: 1`. Mutation-proven by setting `maxSurge: 1`.
- [x] [Review][Patch] `-p:WarningsNotAsErrors=NU1901..NU1904` on four CI restore steps sets a *global* MSBuild property, which a `<PropertyGroup>` cannot override, so `Directory.Build.props:18`'s own definition is inert during CI restore - the two lists are identical today, but anything added there later works locally and is silently dropped in CI. The exception is also widened from the single documented advisory to all four audit codes across the whole restore graph, submodules included, with no allowlist or expiry, and no assertion pins the four steps in sync. [.github/workflows/ci.yml:178] — applied 2026-07-26 as a sync guard rather than a removal: the global property is genuinely required because it is the only way the exception reaches the Hexalith.FrontComposer submodule's separate `Directory.Build.props` chain, so `CiWorkflow_KeepsEveryRestoreAuditExceptionInSyncWithDirectoryBuildProps` now parses the declared list out of `Directory.Build.props`, requires exactly four injected restore steps, and requires each to inject exactly that list. Owner, consequence and reopen trigger are recorded in the test's own comment.
- [x] [Review][Patch] The `sslRootCert` removal is correct but its replacement guard binds nothing - the assertion is now absence plus a comment, and no producer of `sslmode=verify-full` or `sslrootcert` exists anywhere in `deploy/`, `tools/`, or `src/`, so a connection string provisioned with `sslmode=require` leaves every guard green. Bind what is statically bindable and record an explicit accepted blocker for the OpenBao-resident connection-string content with owner, consequence, and reopen trigger. [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests/Deployment/ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs:139] — applied 2026-07-26: what is statically bindable is now bound structurally - `connectionString` must resolve from a `secretKeyRef` (never an inline literal), from secret `access-telemetry-postgresql` key `connectionString`, through `secretStore: access-telemetry-secrets` - and the OpenBao-resident connection-string content is recorded as an accepted blocker in the guard itself, with owner (Hexalith Platform Operations plus the AC4 security reviewer), consequence (gate C1.12 cannot be discharged by any test in this assembly) and reopen trigger. **Chunk-1 review note (2026-07-27).** The diff `c9dfb06f..HEAD` (work since the chunk-3b review) is reviewed in three chunks: Chunk 1 (this pass) covers the ten `AccessTelemetry` Lifecycle adapter source/test paths; Chunk 2 (production-deployment verification tooling) and Chunk 3 (governance/planning docs, including this story file's own sections) are not yet reviewed. No status transition is valid until all three chunks report complete, per `_bmad/custom/story-phase-ledger.md`'s chunked-review rule. Six layers ran (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor, Historical Slice Guard, Story Phase Ledger Auditor); the Acceptance Auditor independently rebuilt and ran the four affected test assemblies (AccessTelemetry.Tests 85/85, Cli.Tests `CiTestInventoryTests` 59/59, Server.Tests `AccessTelemetryRetentionDecisionTests` 10/10, IntegrationTests `AccessTelemetryLifecycleIntegrationCheckpointTests` 8/8) and found no acceptance-criteria violation. 15 raised findings were dismissed after independently reading the actual source (one — a claim that `TransactionalDaprState.Apply` validates and mutates in the same loop — was verified false against the code; the rest were confirmed real but unreachable via the sole production call path, already covered by an existing deferred item, or consistent with an established codebase-wide pattern); see Debug Log References for the full list.

**Chunk-1 resolution (2026-07-27).** All six chunk-1 items (one `[Review][Decision]`, five `[Review][Patch]`) are now applied and checked off. Commits `1f83c4ab` and `000af156` landed independently of this review session while the decision above was being resolved and implemented the decision plus four of the five patches (verified: re-read the actual diff against each finding, then independently rebuilt and ran `Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry.Tests` fresh — `88/88` — and `CiTestInventoryTests` fresh — `59/59`). This code-review phase applied the remaining patch (the C1.11 citation) directly, per the routing its own finding required. Chunk 1 is closed; Chunks 2 and 3 remain, now against HEAD `000af156` (which also touched this story file, `deferred-work.md`, and evidence artifacts outside chunk 1's scope — those fall to Chunk 3).

- [x] [Review][Decision] `InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStore`'s `StaleIndex` delete branch has no post-removal strong verification (unlike its own main `Deleted`/`AlreadyAbsent` branch and unlike `DaprAccessTelemetryStateStore.RemoveBucketEntryAsync`, which re-reads and throws `InvalidOperationException` on failure); separately, even the main branch's existing check can never trigger because this fake has no hook to force an "acknowledged-but-unapplied" state, unlike `TransactionalDaprState`'s `UndeletableKeys`/`UnappliedUpsertKeys`. The class's own doc comment claims to model "a strong post-delete verification that can fail" for parity with the Dapr adapter, but neither branch of this fake can actually fail that verification, so the "portable" checkpoint path can never exercise `VerificationFailed` — the same failure mode is fully covered via the Dapr-fake-based tests instead. Decision needed: invest in closing this test-fidelity gap (add force-failure hooks plus a `StaleIndex`-side check and dedicated tests, mirroring `TransactionalDaprState`), or accept the asymmetry given the class is never registered with the runtime host and the failure mode is already proven elsewhere? [src/Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry/Lifecycle/InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStore.cs:248] — **applied 2026-07-27 per the Administrator decision (close it, and correct the finding).** The finding's second claim was false when raised and is corrected here: `VerificationFailed` *was* already reachable on the main delete branch through the `SuppressRecordDeletion` hook, and `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_BackendKeepsRecordAfterAcknowledgedDelete_ReturnsVerificationFailed` exercised it (passing). The genuine gap was narrower — only the `StaleIndex` branch pruned its entry with no post-removal verification. Closed by mirroring `DaprAccessTelemetryStateStore.RemoveBucketEntryAsync`: a `SuppressEntryRemoval` hook plus a `Seed` helper make the acknowledged-but-unapplied prune constructible, the `StaleIndex` branch now strongly re-reads and throws `InvalidOperationException` when the entry survived, and the prune is guarded on presence so it fires only when the entry was actually held — exactly the Dapr adapter's `if (bucketContainsEntry)` condition. Pinned by the new `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_StalePruneIsAcknowledgedButNotApplied_ThrowsRatherThanReportingStaleIndex`, RED-proven: with the verification removed the class runs `88` with exactly `1` failure — that test — and all 87 pre-existing tests stay green.

The row now cites `...IsDeniedAndLeavesTheRecordPurgeable`, matching live source.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] `CiWorkflow_KeepsEveryRestoreAuditExceptionInSyncWithDirectoryBuildProps` counts only `dotnet restore` invocations that already carry `-p:WarningsNotAsErrors=`; a new restore step added without that flag at all isn't matched, so the count stays at `4` and the guard stays green on exactly the drift its own comment says it exists to catch [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Cli.Tests/Ci/CiTestInventoryTests.cs:427] — applied 2026-07-27: the guard now matches **every** `dotnet restore` invocation (`dotnet restore [^\r\n]*`), requires that total to be exactly `4`, and separately requires every one of them to carry `-p:WarningsNotAsErrors=` before comparing the injected codes. Proven on the finding's own scenario: with a fifth restore step added carrying no flag at all, the **pre-fix** guard passes green (`Total: 1, Failed: 0`) while the fixed guard fails. A second mutation — stripping the flag from one of the four existing steps, keeping the count at `4` — fails the new per-step assertion with its own message.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] `CiWorkflow_RunsEveryToolingFixtureSuiteThatGuardsAShippedTool` has no `.ShouldNotBeEmpty()` guard before its `foreach` over `tests/tooling` subdirectories, so the test would vacuously pass with zero assertions if that directory ever had none; separately its literal `-s tests/tooling/{suite} ` substring match (trailing space) is brittle to harmless workflow-YAML reformatting, though it fails closed (loud failure) rather than silently [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Cli.Tests/Ci/CiTestInventoryTests.cs:460] — applied 2026-07-27: the suite list is materialized and asserted `ShouldNotBeEmpty` before the `foreach`, so the vacuous-pass path is closed. The literal `-s tests/tooling/{suite} ` substring is replaced by `-s\s+tests/tooling/{escaped}(?![0-9A-Za-z_-])`, which tolerates whitespace reformatting while still binding the path as a whole token. Mutation-proven: re-pointing one wired suite to `access_telemetry_lifecycle_typo` fails the guard with `tests/tooling/access_telemetry_lifecycle is executed by no workflow step`, confirming the token boundary rejects a longer name as satisfying the real suite.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] No explicit test pins the `partitionKey`/`ttlInSeconds` metadata contract on delete-path transaction operations — only `WriteRecordAndIndexAsync_CarriesPartitionAndTtlMetadataTheComponentRequires` asserts it, on the write path. The existing green suite implicitly proves the delete path is correct too (`RequireMetadata` throws unconditionally for any operation with wrong metadata), but there is no dedicated documentation-grade assertion for it [tests/Hexalith.Memories.AccessTelemetry.Tests/Lifecycle/DaprAccessTelemetryStateStoreTests.cs:1] — applied 2026-07-27: `DeleteAndVerifyAsync_CarriesPartitionMetadataAndNeverTtlOnAnyOperation` asserts, on the delete transaction specifically, that every operation carries `partitionKey: access-telemetry`, that none carries `ttlInSeconds` (only records expire natively), and that the transaction contains a `Delete`. **Chunk-2 review note (2026-07-27).** Chunk 2 (production-deployment verification tooling: `tools/*.ps1`/`*.py`, their Python/C# tests, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s three new fixture steps, and the `access-telemetry-deployments.yaml` rolling-update fields — 14 files, 2,692 diff lines against `c9dfb06f..HEAD` at `000af156`) is reviewed and closed with this note. All six layers ran again fresh. The Acceptance Auditor independently ran the runnable lanes this diff touches (13/13 `access_telemetry_lifecycle`, 27/27 `production_deployment_evidence`, 31/31 `coverage_gate`, 42/42 `story_scope`, 7/7 `integration_stub_closure`) and cross-checked several claims against a live `kubectl kustomize` render of the production overlay; it also disproved a plausible-looking hypothesis that this diff's OpenBao-variable cleanup caused the currently-red `production-deployment-verification` CI run (run `30246564974`, `DW 27.3-CR17`) — that code was already dead/vestigial before this diff, and the real root cause (`deploy/kubernetes/base/dapr/secretstore.yaml` requiring OpenBao, which the disposable `kind` cluster never gets) predates this whole reviewed range. 20 raised findings were dismissed after independently reading the actual source and, in several cases, executing the code in question (`DECLARED_SINGLE_COMPONENT_FAULT` empty/whitespace bypass — false, `EnvironmentIdentity.from_mapping` already strips and rejects empty required fields before the placeholder-set check ever runs; `canonical_pg_onprem_profile()`'s dependency on the overridable `REVIEWED_KUBE_CONTEXT` — intentional, matches the already-applied 2026-07-26 fix, "fixing" it would regress that fix; the case-insensitive `DELETE`/`PUT`/`PATCH` ACL check — deliberate and documented, narrowly scoped to one Dapr `Configuration` document; a rolling-update outage risk from `maxSurge:0`/`maxUnavailable:1` — moot today, `replicas: 0`; a commented-out `#max_connections` collision and a "CI step silently passes with 0 tests" claim — both verified absent/false against the real manifest and `unittest discover`'s actual exit-5-on-zero-match behavior; plus ~11 minor test/tooling nitpicks consistent with this repo's existing risk tolerance elsewhere). See Debug Log References for the full list.

**Chunk-2 resolution (2026-07-27).** Both decisions and all 6 patches (8 items total) are applied and checked off. The health-evidence fix (scope the succeeded-run body/status contract to each stage's last attempt only) and the fallback-timeout shortening were implemented directly, each backed by a genuine RED-then-GREEN test (`test_succeeded_run_tolerates_a_malformed_earlier_attempt_for_the_same_stage` / `test_succeeded_run_fails_when_the_terminal_attempt_itself_is_malformed`). Verified: `production_deployment_evidence` 30/30, `access_telemetry_lifecycle` 14/14, `coverage_gate` 31/31, `story_scope` 42/42, all fresh. The C# `ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs` changes (a `maxUnavailable` assertion, `ReadYamlSequence` comment tolerance, `ReadHostBasedAuthenticationRules` tab-normalization consistency) could not be built/run in this sandbox (pre-existing `NU1102` on `Hexalith.EventStore.Client`, the same limitation the Acceptance Auditor hit reviewing this chunk) — verified instead by tracing the logic against a fresh `kubectl kustomize` render of the production overlay, confirming no tabs or comment lines exist in current content so both changes are strictly additive. Chunk 2 is closed; Chunk 3 (governance/planning docs) remains.

- [x] [Review][Patch] The new per-attempt health-evidence persistence can make a genuinely successful rollout fail evidence validation. `Save-HealthResponseEvidence` now writes one `health-$stage-$attempt.json` per poll instead of overwriting the previous attempt, and `Wait-AggregateStatus` calls it every ~2 seconds inside its polling loop — including during the `-MeasureFromContainerRunning` window that exists specifically to cover the gap between "container Running" and "app accepting real connections" (`initial-server-health`/`initial-mcp-health` stages). `validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1`'s unchanged succeeded-branch check requires *every* `health-*.json` file to have a parsable JSON body and a status in `(200, 503)` when `verification-result.json` reports `succeeded`. So a single early poll during that startup gap — expected to return a non-JSON transcript or a `$null` status, which is exactly what the gap-measurement exists to tolerate — now survives as its own file and fails the whole evidence-validation step, even though the deployment run legitimately succeeded. Before this diff, that same early-poll file was silently overwritten by the next, successful poll, so only the terminal state was ever validated. **Decision (2026-07-27, Administrator, during this code review): scope the succeeded-branch's strict body/status check to each stage's *last* attempt only** — every attempt stays retained as evidence, but only the terminal attempt per stage must be well-formed JSON with a 200/503 status when the run succeeded. [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:261] [tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1:73] — applied 2026-07-27: the validator now groups health records by stage, tracks each stage's highest `attempt`, and applies the succeeded-run body/status check only to that terminal record; non-terminal attempts are skipped. Proven both directions: `test_succeeded_run_tolerates_a_malformed_earlier_attempt_for_the_same_stage` (an early malformed attempt no longer fails a succeeded run) and `test_succeeded_run_fails_when_the_terminal_attempt_itself_is_malformed` (the terminal attempt is still held to the contract) both pass.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] `verify-production-deployment.ps1`'s netcat fallback now fires on *any* non-zero `wget` exit instead of only when the output contains a `503` line — a real, reasoned fix (the fallback previously missed exactly the slow-start/timeout cases it exists for, per its own updated comment). The cost: every failed poll now costs up to ~11s (5s sleep + 6s `nc` timeout) instead of returning near-immediately. Polls run every ~2 seconds inside `Wait-AggregateStatus`'s `-MeasureFromContainerRunning` loop, and several consecutive early-startup polls typically fail before the app is ready — so as few as 3 slow failed polls (about 57s) can now approach or exceed the stage's own 60-second startup budget, purely from added latency, independent of whether the app itself is starting any slower than before. **Decision (2026-07-27, Administrator, during this code review): shorten the fallback's own timeouts** rather than raising the stage timeout, to keep the per-failed-poll cost low without weakening the 60-second startup budget itself. [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:230] — applied 2026-07-27: `sleep 5`/`nc -w 6` reduced to `sleep 2`/`nc -w 3`, cutting the worst-case per-failed-poll cost from ~11s to ~5s; `production_deployment_evidence_test.py`'s pinned `nc -w 6` assertion updated to `nc -w 3` to match.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] The "Production-Shaped Execution Contract" spec section still reads "Any target other than `jpiquot@local/hexalith-memories` ... fails C1," but the code this diff's range ships no longer gates on `kube_context` at all — an already-approved 2026-07-26 decision (recorded at this story's own line 194) made the reviewed context overridable via `REVIEWED_KUBE_CONTEXT` and recorded only as an observation (`kube_context_is_reviewed_label`), never a fail-closed gate. The frozen prose was never reconciled to the code it describes [_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/27-3-production-adapter-and-deployment-profile.md:373] — applied 2026-07-27: the sentence now states the client-side context is an observation, not a gate, and names the actual binding identity (`KUBE_NAMESPACE` + `PROFILE_ID`).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] The `maxUnavailable: 1` code comment in the lifecycle Deployment claims "`ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests` reads both operands out of the rendered overlay and fails if either drifts," but the test only reads `replicas` and `maxSurge` via `ReadIntegerField` — `maxUnavailable` itself is never read or asserted anywhere in the repo. The fix is protected against a verbatim revert, not against the underlying scenario recurring in a differently-written form [tests/tooling/production_deployment_evidence/production_deployment_evidence_test.py:118] — applied 2026-07-27: `test_redaction_at_write_does_not_corrupt_the_health_decision` extracts the real `Protect-EvidenceText` function, sets `HEXALITH_ZOT_USERNAME` to the literal value `Healthy` (the exact status string being decided on), and proves the raw parse still returns `status=Healthy` while `Protect-EvidenceText` applied separately still redacts it to `***`.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] The hand-rolled YAML-parsing helpers in `ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs` rest on several unstated assumptions about the current rendering shape that none of the real manifests currently violate but each is a latent trap for the next manifest edit: `ReadYamlSequence` requires its key to occur exactly once and has no comment-line tolerance inside a sequence block; `ReadComponentMetadataSecretRef` locates `secretKeyRef:` via an exact block-style string match with no flow-style fallback; `ReadHostBasedAuthenticationRules`'s tab-to-space normalization isn't applied to the sibling generic parsers or to its own indent/dedent check; and `ReadServerParameterInt32`'s whole-rendered-manifest scan would collide with an unrelated component defining the same parameter name elsewhere in the overlay [tests/Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests/Deployment/ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests.cs:533] — partially applied 2026-07-27: `ReadYamlSequence` now tolerates a `#`-prefixed comment line at or past the sequence's own indent instead of ending the block, and `ReadHostBasedAuthenticationRules` normalizes tabs before computing indent (not only when building the comparison string), so the dedent check and the rule text now operate on the same basis. Verified against a fresh `kubectl kustomize` render: no tabs and no comment lines exist in the current `pg_hba.conf`/RBAC/ACL content, so both changes are strictly additive for today's manifests. The C# assembly itself could not be rebuilt/run in this sandbox (pre-existing `NU1102` on `Hexalith.EventStore.Client`, unrelated to this diff — the same limitation the Acceptance Auditor hit reviewing this same chunk), so this was verified by tracing the logic against the real rendered manifest rather than by an executed test run. The flow-style `secretKeyRef:` fallback and the whole-manifest `ReadServerParameterInt32` scoping were left as accepted residual risk — neither is reachable against any current manifest, and a proper fix would mean hand-writing more YAML-shape-specific parsing rather than a narrow patch.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] The new "Run access-telemetry lifecycle C1 fixtures" CI step pins the exact filename `test_adapter_profile.py`, unlike its two sibling steps added in the same hunk which use the `*_test.py` wildcard. `tests/tooling/access_telemetry_lifecycle/` currently holds only that one file, so nothing is missed today, but a second fixture file added later (following either naming convention used elsewhere in this repo) would silently never run in CI — `unittest discover`'s fail-closed behavior (confirmed: exit code `5`, "NO TESTS RAN") only triggers when *zero* files match, not when some are missed [.github/workflows/ci.yml:235] — applied 2026-07-27: the pattern is now `test_*.py`, matching this directory's own naming convention; verified it still discovers the same 13 tests.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] Several minor gaps in the manually-run (not CI-invoked) `verify_access_telemetry_lifecycle.py`/`verify-access-telemetry-lifecycle.py` C1 checkpoint CLI: `_run_command` doesn't catch `UnicodeDecodeError` alongside subprocess errors; `_redact` matches only keyword-adjacent secrets, not unlabeled high-entropy tokens; the pre/post `workload_identity` comparison reports "unchanged" even when both observations are empty (vacuously "unchanged"); and `daprd` version probing gives up after the first `Running` pod lacks the container rather than trying the next one. All affect only the evidence quality of a human-supervised checkpoint run, not an automated gate [tools/verify_access_telemetry_lifecycle.py:1950] — applied 2026-07-27: `_run_command`'s `subprocess.run` now passes `errors="replace"` so invalid-UTF-8 output becomes a recorded observation rather than an escaping `UnicodeDecodeError` (new test `test_invalid_utf8_output_becomes_an_observation_not_a_traceback`, 14/14 green); the workload-identity comparison now reports `not observed` rather than a vacuous `true` when both sides are empty; and daprd version probing now tries every `Running` pod in turn instead of stopping at the first. The unlabeled high-entropy-secret detection in `_redact` was left as accepted residual risk — a robust version is a real feature (risking over-redaction of legitimate hashes/IDs), not a narrow patch, and this only affects the richness of a human-reviewed diagnostic packet.

- [x] [Review][Patch] [High] Re-derive and re-document the per-poll latency bound against the unchanged 60-second startup budget — the diff deleted the comment that tied the numbers to the budget ("worst case ~5s, not ~11s: polling happens every ~2s against a 60s startup budget") while raising the in-container fallback from `sleep 2`/`nc -w 3` to `sleep 4`/`nc -w 8` and adding `curl --max-time 10 --retry 3 --retry-delay 1`, whose max-time resets per retry (4 × 10s + 3 × 1s = 43s). - [x] [Review][Patch] [High] Use the pod's `$APP_API_TOKEN` in the fallback and constrain which status codes may overwrite the in-container observation — `:240` hardcodes `dapr-api-token: verification-app-api-token`, duplicating the seeded literal at `:651`, while the in-container probe deliberately reads `${APP_API_TOKEN}` so it matches the Kubernetes probes. `:305-308` then accepts any 3-digit code. A pod whose token is wrong or unmounted returns 401 in-container, the fallback fires with the correct literal, and the stage passes — making the app-token authentication contract unfalsifiable by this lane. - [x] [Review][Patch] [High] Derive the disclosure from the cluster rather than asserting it — `originalType` is a literal, nothing reads the components' pre-patch `spec.type`, and nothing re-reads them afterward to confirm the merge took effect (`Invoke-Checked` proves only kubectl exit 0; a no-op patch also exits 0). The validator then asserts the same literal back, so the check is a tautology: dropping `Invoke-Checked` from both patch calls lets them fail silently while every test stays green. The two component names are additionally hardcoded in four places (patch args, disclosure, validator predicate, test fixture) with no enumeration by `spec.type`, so a third vault-typed component would be silently unpatched and still pass [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:679] — applied 2026-07-29 by code review (chunk 2).

This is a regression introduced by the 2026-07-31 interpolation rewrite — the prior inline version used a single-quoted string. The validator checks only `IsNullOrWhiteSpace` (`tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1:167-169`), so raw control characters and nonexistent component names ship in the uploaded evidence packet, defeating the stated purpose of the same sentence ("the auditor otherwise cannot tell which component is at risk"). Compounding it, the narrative is pinned by no test at all: deleting the entire restored sentence leaves `Ran 102 tests / OK` (verification-gap mutation M4) — the second time this exact content has been lost. Fix: use a single-quoted format string or double the backticks, and assert in `test_two_vault_components_are_substituted_and_disclosed` that the produced `reason` names each component in `substitutedComponents` and mentions `memories-access-telemetry-secret-reader`. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [High] "The disclosure is now written on every path" is false for two throwing paths that leave a rewritten cluster [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:154] — the comment at `:163-168` claims every throw between the patch loop and the disclosure write was converted into a collected failure, but two `Invoke-Checked` calls still throw before the write at `:244`: `:154` (`kubectl patch component` inside the `foreach`, so components 1..N-1 are already rewritten when N fails) and `:172` (`$observedJson = (Invoke-Checked kubectl @('get', ...))`, which sits **outside** the `try` at `:173` — that `try` wraps only `ConvertFrom-Json`). Reviewer-confirmed by reading the block. In both cases `secret-store-substitution.json` is never written, the packet is `failed`, and `tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1:267` skips the disclosure branch entirely, so the packet validates clean over a silently mutated cluster — the precise hole the redesign claims to have closed. No test can observe either path: the stubbed `kubectl` in `SecretStoreSubstitutionExecutionTests` always exits 0. Fix: wrap the patch loop and the post-patch `Invoke-Checked` so failures land in `$substitutionFailures` instead of propagating, making the `:244` write unconditionally reachable. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).

- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] The per-poll budget test reads the curl timeout from a comment, not from the executed command [tests/tooling/production_deployment_evidence/production_deployment_evidence_test.py:1361] — the regex `r"--max-time', '(\d+)'|--max-time (\d+)"` with `re.search` returns the first match in the file. - [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] `Get-HealthResponseViaPortForward` has no execution test; inverting its established-branch guard leaves the suite green [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:522] — the diff restructured the established/not-established split so `curl` runs under `if ($established)`, yet all 12 `extract_ps_function` call sites target other functions and `production_deployment_evidence_test.py:1138` stubs this function out inside `GetHealthResponseExecutionTests`; its only coverage is source-text `assertIn`. Verification-gap mutations: `if ($established)` → `if (-not $established)` gives `Ran 102 tests / OK`, and moving `$forwardError = Get-CapturedProcessText` back under `if (-not $established)` gives `Ran 102 tests / OK`. Fix: drive the real function as `DisposableClusterGuardExecutionTests` does — a stub `kubectl` on PATH that sleeps while a stub `curl` prints a canned `HTTP/1.1 503` plus body — and assert the transcript carries the body on the established path. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).

- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] Four rules in `Assert-SubstitutionDisclosureShape` are unexercised and can be disabled with the suite green [tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1:182] — verification-gap guarded each `if` with `$false -and …` in turn: `:182-184` (`substitutionPerformed=true` naming no component), `:201-203` (cardinality), `:211-213` (the `observedComponents.Count -gt 0` half of the unmodified-run contradiction check), and `:254-257` (non-empty `verificationFailures` on a succeeded run). - [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] The startup-budget credit fix was applied to only one of the two branches, so the 2x ceiling survives on the failure path [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:810] — `:789-797` identifies the defect as bounding the CREDIT rather than the RESULT and removes the credit on the healthy branch at `:798`, but `:810-812` still computes `$failureOverhead = [math]::Min($probeOverheadSeconds, $TimeoutSeconds)` and subtracts it, preserving exactly the shape the comment says was removed. Edge-case layer executed the real `Wait-AggregateStatus` with `timeout_seconds=10, started_ago=19, fallback=5000, status Unhealthy`: the failure branch did not throw (19 − 10 = 9 ≤ 10). Scaled to production values a container stuck Unhealthy for up to 119s does not trip the 60-second startup throw, while one that becomes Healthy at 119s fails immediately — two different contracts from one `-TimeoutSeconds 60`. `StartupBudgetContractTests.test_a_119_second_start_fails_the_60_second_contract` exercises only the Healthy/200 path. This is the residual of the Administrator's 2026-07-31 ruling at `:563` to cap the effective total at 60s. Fix: drop the credit on the failure branch too, and cover the never-healthy path. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] The per-poll worst-case model omits the only genuinely unbounded call in the poll path [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:605] — the in-container probe `& kubectl exec -n $namespace $Pod -c $Container -- /bin/sh -ec $probeCommand` carries no `--request-timeout`, and `Get-HealthResponse` calls it on every poll (`:753`). The comment at `:453-456` asserts "this exec was the one unbounded term in the poll budget", which is false. `production_deployment_evidence_test.py:1364` matches `kubectl --request-timeout=(\d+)s exec`, resolving only to the token exec at `:457`, and `Get-RunningContainerObservation`'s per-poll `kubectl --request-timeout=15s get pods` (`:327`, called at `:729`) is absent from `runner_worst_case` entirely. `wget -T 6` / `nc -w 4` bound the command inside the container, not the exec stream, so an API server that accepts the connection but never returns blocks the poll indefinitely while the class reports 37s. Fix: add `--request-timeout=15s` to `:605` and include both it and the `get pods` term in `runner_worst_case`. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] A response whose status code the allow-list rejects still supplies the authoritative body [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:642] — the fallback body is adopted first and `$fallbackStatusCode` is filtered through `@(200, 503)` only afterwards. Blind-hunter executed the real function with the new test's own fixture (in-container 200 plus non-JSON, fallback 401 carrying `{"status":"Healthy"}`) and got `status_code: 200`, `body: {"schemaVersion":1,"status":"Healthy"}`. `Wait-AggregateStatus:771` gates on `$health.status -eq $ExpectedStatus -and $lastStatusCode -eq $expectedHttpStatus`, both of which hold, so a stage passes on a body served by a response the allow-list explicitly refused. `production_deployment_evidence_test.py:1193` constructs exactly this scenario and asserts only `status_code`, certifying the guard while its own data demonstrates the hole. Fix: reject the body when its status code fails the allow-list, and extend `:1193` to assert the body. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] `Get-PodApplicationToken`'s `<unavailable: …>` markers never reach any evidence artifact [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:459] — `:459` and `:467` are the only occurrences of the marker, and its sole consumer is the request header at `:528`. `curl -sS -D -` dumps response headers only, and nothing adds the marker to `$transcript`. A pod with `APP_API_TOKEN` unset or an exec that fails answers 401, which `Get-HealthResponse`'s allow-list at `:654` silently discards, so no evidence file distinguishes "no token" from "wrong token" from "pod never answered" — the exact state the comment at `:462-465` claims to have fixed ("Every branch emits a marker"). `PodApplicationTokenExecutionTests` pins the return values only. Fix: append the marker to `$transcript` before the curl call, and assert it is observable downstream. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] `substitutionVerified`, `verificationFailures`, and `residualVaultComponents` are entirely unconstrained on a failed run [tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1:277] — the failed branch calls only `Assert-SubstitutionDisclosureShape`, which never touches those fields; the type check (`:251`) and the failures/residual checks (`:254`, `:258`) live in the succeeded-only block. Edge-case layer executed a `status: failed` packet with `substitutionVerified: "definitely-not-a-boolean"`, `verificationFailures: []`, and `residualVaultComponents: ["secretstore","access-telemetry-secrets"]` — validated `rc=0`. This contradicts the stated contract at `:268-270` ("Validate the SAME shape as a succeeded run — only `substitutionVerified` may be false here"). Fix inside the shape function: require `substitutionVerified -is [bool]`, and require it false whenever `verificationFailures` or `residualVaultComponents` is non-empty. Must be sequenced after the `[Review][Decision]` on the failed-run contract, whose chosen arm sets the relationship between `observedComponents` and `substitutedComponents`. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Medium] The sole positive failed-run fixture asserts a packet the producer cannot emit [tests/tooling/production_deployment_evidence/production_deployment_evidence_test.py:2068] — `test_a_failed_run_may_legitimately_record_an_unverified_substitution` hand-writes `observedComponents` fully populated *and* `residualVaultComponents: ["secretstore"]` *and* `verificationFailures` naming `secretstore` as vault-typed. Both work only because neither donor class defines `setUp`; adding one later silently skips it for the borrowing class, and the unbound call breaks the moment `drive` reads instance state — presenting as an unrelated test error. Fix: extract the shared drivers into module-level helpers. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] The shared output helper joins two streams without a separator and can strip legitimate content [tests/tooling/production_deployment_evidence/production_deployment_evidence_test.py:112] — `combined = (result.stdout or "") + (result.stderr or "")` concatenates the last stdout line directly onto the first stderr line before whitespace collapsing, so an assertion can match across the boundary of two unrelated streams. The gutter regex `^\s*(?:\d+\s*)?\|\s?` also strips any line beginning with digits followed by `|`, a plausible shape for validator diagnostic output. Every new negative assertion in this diff routes through this helper, so both directions of imprecision are load-bearing. Fix: join with a newline and narrow the gutter regex to the PowerShell error-context shape it targets. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] The owner-check guard discards the diagnosis for its own refusal and is bypassed entirely by a missing binary [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:92] — `:92` reads the context with `2>$null` and `:93-95` reports only "could not read the active kubectl context", so the operator loses whether it was a missing kubeconfig, an RBAC denial, or a corrupt context file, on the one guard standing between the script and `kubectl patch component … spec.type`. If `kubectl` is not on `PATH`, `& kubectl` raises a terminating `CommandNotFoundException` and the refusal message never appears. `test_guard_refuses_when_the_context_cannot_be_read` covers only exit-1-with-empty-stdout. Fix: capture stderr into the refusal message and wrap the invocation so a missing binary refuses with the guard's own error. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).
- [x] [Review][Patch] [Low] Temp capture files leak silently on exactly the path that already emits a kill marker [tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1:553] — `:431-435` returns the `<unreadable: …>` marker precisely when the still-running `kubectl port-forward` holds the handle, and `:553-555` then deletes the same two paths with `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue`, recording nothing. Two temp files per poll leak in `[System.IO.Path]::GetTempPath()` for the duration of a 4-minute stage times every stage, with no trace in the packet. Fix: mirror the kill marker — record `capture file not removed: <path>` in the transcript when the file survives the delete. — applied 2026-07-31 by code review (second pass).

## Implementation Checkpoints

Story 27.3 is an explicitly approved C0 plus C2/C3/C4 verification story after the 2026-07-30 all-C1 transfer. Every retained evidence row is mandatory. The C1 umbrella is closed only as an administrative transfer record; it qualifies no Production adapter behavior and never closes A41.

**C2 added 2026-07-27 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-27 (DW 27.3-CR15).** C2 proves AC6 and is independent of C1: it is not gated on the five external C1 inputs, and passing it advances no C1 gate and does not enable Production lifecycle writes. AC5 continues to apply unchanged. The four `.tar.gz` archives the lane consumes are produced by `tools/publish-containers.ps1`, which the 2026-07-27 Epic 30 split assigns to **Story 30.3**; that story must not regress this lane. The forward dependency recorded earlier in this story stands with its owner updated from Story 30.1 to Story 30.3.

**Dependency correction 2026-08-03.** The preceding AC5 reference and Story 30.1/30.3 dependency attribution are retained as superseded history and are non-binding. Story 27.3 consumes the checked-in `tools/publish-containers.ps1` local-build contract through its own `production-deployment-verification` job at the same reviewed source SHA. Story 30.3 need not reach any status first: it owns future registry-publication hardening and non-regression of the four stable archive names and their ability to feed this lane. Its `backlog` status and Hexalith.Builds activation gate remain independent.

### Historical C3/C4 Establishment Record (Non-Binding C1 Attribution)

**C3 and C4 added 2026-07-27 by code review, per the Administrator decision of that date (option 1), executing the 2026-07-26 decision recorded in `Review Findings`.** That decision was that each of the three independently demonstrable lanes gains its own checkpoint row with individual owner, evidence command, review state and completion state; only the deployment-verification lane (C2) had received one, so the PG-v2 adapter/unit lane and the PG-ONPREM-1 manifest-guard lane were shipping, passing and closing findings on their own runners with nothing able to record it while every other row stayed gated behind the externally blocked C1. Both are marked complete on executed evidence from 2026-07-27. **Neither advances C1, enables Production lifecycle writes, unblocks Story 27.4, nor mutates A41**: they are unit and static-manifest lanes, and every C1 child-gate row remains `pending` / `not complete` under Story 27.5.

**C1 attribution corrected 2026-08-03.** The preceding Story 27.5 attribution is preserved only as non-binding history from the C3/C4 establishment phase. Story 27.5 was withdrawn by the approved 2026-08-01 correction; this record confers no current C1 ownership or completion authority. C3 and C4 remain binding only through AC7/AC8 and the current C0/C2/C3/C4 checkpoint table below.

**Ratified 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28**, executing the Administrator decision of 2026-07-27 (eighth-invocation review, option a): C3 and C4 are ratified through `correct-course` plus the matching `epics.md` amendment, matching the C2/AC6 precedent. Each now has a declaring acceptance criterion — **C3 proves AC7** and **C4 proves AC8** — added to both `epics.md` and this story's `## Acceptance Criteria` by that proposal. The sentence previously here, "No `epics.md` amendment is required, per the same decision", is superseded and removed: the 2026-07-27 review found that C3 and C4 had been created and marked `complete` with no approved sprint change and no `epics.md` entry, whereas the immediately preceding C2/AC6 carried both. Neither row's evidence, review state, completion state, nor completion date changes at this ratification; both keep their 2026-07-27 re-citation under the amended **Checkpoint Execution Contract** below.

### Checkpoint Execution Contract

Every C# checkpoint command below is a two-stage command contract: first build the named test project from the reviewed source with `DOTNET_CLI_USE_MSBUILD_SERVER=0 dotnet build <project> --configuration Release --disable-build-servers -m:1 /nr:false -p:NuGetAudit=false -p:MinVerVersionOverride=1.0.0`; then hash the reviewed source/profile and fresh assembly, discover the exact selected classes with `DiffEngine_Disabled=true dotnet exec <assembly> -list methods -noLogo`, and execute that same assembly with the table's selectors, `-parallel none -noLogo`. Immediately after execution, re-hash the source/profile and assembly and require byte-for-byte equality with the pre-execution values. The evidence must prove every selector discovers at least one unique method, that the executed **case** count is greater than zero and maps onto the selected **method** discovery count, and that `Errors`, `Failed`, and `Not Run` are all zero. A stale or concurrently changed source/profile/assembly, missing selector, zero discovery, unexplained count mismatch, or nonzero result fails the checkpoint even when the process exit code is zero.

**Amended 2026-07-27 by the Administrator during code review (eighth-invocation review).** The clause previously required `Total` to *equal* the selected discovery count and `Skipped` to be zero. Both were unsatisfiable in practice and the review found C3 and C4 marked `complete` on evidence the clause rejected. Two carve-outs are now explicit, and neither weakens the fail-closed intent:

- **Methods versus cases.** `dotnet exec -list methods` counts test *methods*; execution reports test *cases*, and one `[Theory]` method yields many. `Total == discovery count` therefore holds only for an assembly with no theories - true of C0 (`8/8`) by accident, false for any real lane. The requirement is now that `Total >= discovered methods > 0` and that the difference is attributable to `[Theory]` expansion; a `Total` **below** the discovered method count still fails, because that means methods did not run.
- **Pre-existing named skips.** A skip that is declared, owned, and unrelated to the checkpoint under proof does not fail it, provided the row names the skipped test and its owner. An undeclared or checkpoint-relevant skip still fails. `Not Run` remains strictly zero, since that indicates selection or execution failure rather than a declared exclusion.

Rows completed before this amendment must re-cite their evidence against it rather than inherit a pass.

#### Production-Shaped Execution Contract

Set non-empty `KUBE_CONTEXT`, `KUBE_NAMESPACE`, `DEPLOYMENT_ID`, `PROFILE_ID`, `EVIDENCE_ROOT`, and `DECLARED_SINGLE_COMPONENT_FAULT` values for the reviewed `PG-ONPREM-1` environment. The verifier must record its expanded `kubectl`/Dapr/backend-evidence child commands, stdout, stderr, exit codes, UTC timestamps, pre/post workload identities, source/profile hashes, and immutable artifact hashes. **Corrected 2026-07-27 by code review (chunk 2):** a client-side `kube_context` name is not an identity control (per the 2026-07-26 decision at line 194) and is recorded only as the `kube_context_is_reviewed_label` observation, never a fail-closed gate; the binding identity is `KUBE_NAMESPACE` plus `PROFILE_ID`. A mocked control plane, missing expansion transcript, skipped fault/probe, or zero discovered observation still fails C1.

```bash
python3 tools/verify-access-telemetry-lifecycle.py --checkpoint adapter-profile --kube-context "$KUBE_CONTEXT" --namespace "$KUBE_NAMESPACE" --deployment-id "$DEPLOYMENT_ID" --profile-id "$PROFILE_ID" --workload-profile adr-27.1-two-writer-500eps --steady-state-minutes 30 --purge-backlog-records 150000 --evidence "$EVIDENCE_ROOT/27-3-adapter-profile-evidence.md"
```

### Current C2 Unblock Sequence

Execute these steps in order; a later green step never waives an earlier failure.

1. Close C0 first: the Story 27.2 owner resolves `DW 27.3-CR42` through `DW 27.3-CR46`
   on executed evidence, then Story 27.3 rebuilds/discovers/runs the canonical 8-method selector
   and the code reviewer re-closes C0.
2. Freeze the reviewed source and record its exact commit SHA. It must contain the current
   verifier, validator, production-deployment fixtures, identical AC6 copies, and this story
   record; dirty-worktree-only content is not CI evidence.
3. Run the local evidence-tool contract:

   ```bash
   PYTHONHASHSEED=0 python3 -m unittest discover -s tests/tooling/production_deployment_evidence -p '*_test.py' -v
   ```

   Require the full discovered inventory with zero failures, errors, or skips.
4. Run C4's exact Release build, discovery, and
   `Hexalith.Memories.Server.Tests.Deployment.ProductionDeploymentArtifactsTests` selector under
   the Checkpoint Execution Contract. Require a fresh assembly, nonzero discovery, and zero
   errors/failures/not-run.
5. After separate Git authorization, make that exact reviewed SHA available to CI on a branch
   for which `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs. This step does not itself authorize commit or push.
6. Run `production-deployment-verification`. Its ordered job steps must all execute: checkout;
   build-submodule/.NET initialization; kubectl; kind; Dapr CLI; four-archive Release publication;
   disposable rollout verification; evidence validation (`if: always()`); evidence upload
   (`if: always()`). Require the run head SHA to equal the reviewed SHA, job conclusion `success`,
   and publication/verification/validation/upload each present and `success`, never skipped or
   cancelled.
7. Record `<run-id>`, `<job-id>`, and the `production-deployment-evidence` `<artifact-id>`, then
   retrieve through the API rather than `gh run download`:

   ```bash
   gh api repos/:owner/:repo/actions/runs/<run-id>/artifacts
   gh api repos/:owner/:repo/actions/artifacts/<artifact-id>/zip > evidence.zip
   ```

8. Extract into a fresh directory and run the reviewed
   `tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1` against
   `artifacts/production-deployment-verification`. Require exit `0`, a succeeded terminal
   `required-server-mcp-restored` result, schema-v2 substitution disclosure, non-empty Component
   enumeration, per-component readback when substituted, authenticated 200 and 503 health packets,
   current/previous logs, and all four archives.
9. Only the owning code-review phase may close C2. It independently repeats retrieval/validation,
   replaces the blocked cells with `reviewed <date>`, `complete`, and a bare ISO completion date,
   and records the reviewed SHA plus run/job/artifact IDs. An uploaded artifact from a failed
   verifier or validator never closes C2.
10. Run the story slice, File Scope, and review-readiness gates over the literal governed changed
    set. Advance to `review` only if C0 and C2 are complete, all other before-review obligations
    are closed, and the final readiness command exits `0`.

### Historical C1 Gate Evidence Record (Non-Binding)

**Table removed 2026-07-30 by code review.** This section retains its normative preamble as append-only history, but it governs NO rows: the twelve retained child-gate rows were transferred with C1.13 and C1.15-C1.25. Read every "row below" statement here as history describing the pre-transfer table, not as a live obligation on Story 27.3. The current 25-row contract is held in Annex A of `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/sprint-change-proposal-2026-07-30.md`. **[Superseded 2026-08-01 by code review (second pass): Annex A's own header now reads "superseded 2026-07-31 ... no longer a live contract". The live 25-row contract is registered in `epics.md` under Stories 27.5 and 27.6.]**

Every row below is an independent C1 gate and must pass on its own evidence. Per `epics.md` checkpoint-guard policy one row per gate is required, a gate cannot be marked complete because the C1 umbrella row is complete, and per AC5 any row not in `passed` completion state keeps Production writes disabled, Story 27.3 `in-progress`, Story 27.4 `backlog`, and A41 open. All rows begin `pending` review state and `not complete` completion state; no completion is claimed by adding or extending this table.

**Transition rule (added 2026-07-26 by code review, chunk 3b; amended 2026-07-30 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-30).** A child-gate row leaves `not complete` only when its named observation exists in the referenced evidence packet with `evidence_is_approval: true`, its accountable owner records the transition, and the code reviewer confirms the observation against the packet. `passed` is the only admissible child-gate completion state; `pending` review state and `passed` completion state cannot coexist. A child gate whose named producer cannot emit its observation stays `not complete` regardless of any other evidence. The Story 27.3 C1 umbrella's administrative closure is not evidence, is not `passed`, and cannot discharge any Story 27.5 child gate.

**Capacity admission (added 2026-07-26 by code review, chunk 3b, per the Administrator decision).** The approved 2026-07-20 course correction's threshold table governs admission: steady state <=70% (300,647,710,720 bytes), reclamation peak <=80%, critical at 80% (343,597,383,680), Unhealthy at 90% (386,547,056,640). Where the narrative elsewhere in this story reads that a duration is admissible if it "fits the exact 400 GiB profile", the threshold table is the controlling gate and 100% occupancy is not admissible. A measured result at exactly 80% is treated as critical, not as an admissible peak. If the configured 24-hour target itself fails the 70% steady-state threshold, `PG-ONPREM-1` is rejected for that retention duration and a new correct-course decision is required; a passing 1-hour horizon licenses nothing on its own. Amending Task 1's own wording is a task-definition change and remains with the story owner.


**Producer correction 2026-07-27 by code review, per the Administrator decision of that date (option 2).** Thirteen rows (C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14) cited the shared `adapter-profile` command as their producer, while `tools/verify_access_telemetry_lifecycle.py` states it is "NOT a behavioural prober" and that "each of those rows names its own operator-executed command in the story's C1 Gate Evidence Table" - a cross-reference resolving in neither direction, because the table named no such command. Only the docstring half of the 2026-07-26 decision had been applied. Those thirteen rows are now marked **blocked** rather than given commands nobody can run: no operator-executable producer can exist for a CRUD, strong-read, ETag, transaction-fault, TTL, actor/Scheduler, request-bound, throughput, purge-backlog, isolation, encryption or reclamation observation while the Production lifecycle environment is disabled. Authoring thirteen unrunnable, unvalidatable commands would be exactly the paper evidence this story has refused throughout. **Owner:** Deployment adapter owner plus Hexalith Platform Operations, and the independent security reviewer for C1.11/C1.12. **Consequence:** these thirteen gates cannot be discharged by any artifact that exists today; they stay `pending` / `not complete`, so AC5 keeps Production writes disabled, Story 27.4 `backlog` and A41 open. **Reopen trigger:** the `PG-ONPREM-1` lifecycle Deployments are scaled above zero with the production flag and profile hash set and the clock authorities pointed at real endpoints; each row's own operator-executed command is then authored against the running target and recorded here before any completion state changes.

## Dev Notes

### Scope and Authority

- The exact Story/AC text comes from current `epics.md`; the accepted ADR and its **Production Adapter Qualification — PG-ONPREM-1** appendix are the technical authority. Story 27.2 implements portable runtime behavior; Story 27.3 qualifies the exact Production adapter and immutable deployment profile; Story 27.4 owns deployment-shaped lifecycle evidence, operations guidance, and the remaining A41 close-out. [Source: _bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md#Story-27.3-Production-Adapter-and-Deployment-Profile; docs/dev/adr-27.1-001-access-telemetry-lifecycle.md#Production-Adapter-Qualification--PG-ONPREM-1]
- This story has one independently demonstrable outcome: an immutable, fully probed, configured-capacity-funded, backup/restore-documented, and separately approved `PG-ONPREM-1` C1 packet. It does not claim lifecycle close-out or mutate A41. **Narrowed 2026-07-28 by approved Sprint Change Proposal 2026-07-28:** "fully probed" means the twelve retained C1 gate rows (C1.13 and C1.15-C1.25) plus the C2, C3 and C4 lanes; the thirteen capability gates that can only be observed against the running target (C1.1-C1.12 and C1.14) are Story 27.5's, and this story neither implements nor claims them. Every retained checkpoint names an owner, an evidence command or artifact, a review state, and a completion state, which is the `story-scope-guard.md:41-43` condition for remaining one tracking story. *(Citation corrected 2026-07-31 by code review: `:32-34` is a blank line plus "Select work from current epic intent and current code evidence, not numeric story adjacency", not the umbrella-checkpoint condition described here.)* **Superseded 2026-07-31 by code review, per the approved 2026-07-30 correction:** Story 27.3 owns no C1 child gate at all, so the single demonstrable outcome stated at the head of this bullet is append-only history. The retained scope is C0 and C2-C4 plus this story's governed remediation and ledger obligations; the retarget of the title and `## Story` statement is `DW 27.3-CR35`.
- Run another correct-course decision if `PG-ONPREM-1` is rejected or discovery requires multiple adapters, a multi-node/replicated-storage platform, a general trusted-time/persistence/operations platform, a tenant-facing inspection product, or an independently deployable raw-console/OTLP privacy change. Keep writes disabled and A41 open.
- Bounded infrastructure telemetry remains separate from domain persistence. Do not add access telemetry to Hexalith.EventStore, repurpose domain state, or add direct infrastructure dependencies to product/lifecycle code.

### Architecture and Technology Guardrails

- Stable logical identities remain `memories-access-telemetry`, `AccessTelemetryLifecycleActor/global`, `memories-access-telemetry-clock`, `access-telemetry-store`, `access-telemetry-secrets`, and `access-telemetry-config` unless an accepted ADR amendment says otherwise.
- Repository pins control implementation: .NET SDK 10.0.302/net10.0/C# 14, Dapr .NET packages 1.18.4, Aspire AppHost SDK 13.4.6, StackExchange.Redis 3.0.17, xUnit v3 3.2.2, Shouldly 4.3.0, and NSubstitute 5.3.0. Do not upgrade dependencies in this story. The running Dapr runtime/component identity must be captured independently.
- Use current one-type-per-file, file-scoped namespace, XML documentation, source-generated logging, central package management, warnings-as-errors, `.slnx`, SDK containers, and existing composition roots.
- Preserve sanitization-before-enqueue, finite catalogs, bounded queues/batches/retries, signed independent UTC, source-age retention, transactional record/index writes, fixed-actor/reminder recovery, separate authorities, low-cardinality metrics, and business-readiness isolation.

### Latest Technical Information

Research snapshot: 2026-07-18. Repository pins remain authoritative.

- Dapr state-store feature tables are not behavioral certification. The official Dapr 1.18 Redis implementation states that Redis transactions lack rollback; the exact adapter must pass a partial-commit fault probe. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/reference/components-reference/supported-state-stores/; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/dapr/components-contrib/v1.18.0/state/redis/redis.go]
- Dapr forwards `ttlInSeconds` to native stores but does not independently expire state; logical expiry and active purge remain authoritative and testable separately. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/developing-applications/building-blocks/state-management/state-store-ttl/]
- Actor reminders survive activation failover through Dapr Scheduler; Production evidence must cover Scheduler/Placement persistence and replacement, not only the actor process. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/developing-applications/building-blocks/actors/actors-timers-reminders/; https://docs.dapr.io/operations/hosting/kubernetes/kubernetes-persisting-scheduler/]
- Dapr metadata and health APIs expose runtime/component/actor/Scheduler identity needed in the evidence packet; do not infer runtime version from application packages. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/reference/api/metadata_api/; https://docs.dapr.io/reference/api/health_api/]
- Actor state-store component changes require restart and component reload can transiently close/reinitialize the component; rollout/rollback must preserve manifests and prove epoch convergence. [Source: https://docs.dapr.io/operations/components/component-updates/; https://docs.dapr.io/reference/api/configuration_api/]
- Redis `everysec` AOF and `WAIT` do not establish a universal zero-loss guarantee. If Redis is selected, scope the tested fault/RPO precisely and separately prove dataset and AOF/disk reclamation for the test cohort. [Source: https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/persistence/; https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/wait/; https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/memory-usage/; https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/info/]
- The current local integration environment reported Dapr runtime 1.18.1 while application Dapr packages are 1.18.4. This is baseline evidence of identity drift, not the Production identity; C1 must capture and pin the running Production runtime/component profile explicitly.

The kind-cluster-specific cause is therefore unresolved and is not guessed at in this record. - 2026-07-28 (ninth dev-story invocation, continued): **second C2 repair iteration — instrument the silent path, make the body capture deterministic.** Two changes to `tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1`, both inside this story's declared scope. (1) The in-container netcat branch now prints `nc-fallback begin (wget exit N)` / `nc-fallback end (nc exit N)` markers and merges nc's stderr, so a silent transcript can no longer be confused with a skipped branch, and its patience is raised to `sleep 4`/`nc -w 8` (the shortened timeouts were exonerated by the 6s-delay reproduction but the longer window costs nothing outside failed polls). (2) A runner-side `Get-HealthResponseViaPortForward` last-resort: when neither capture path yielded a status-bearing JSON object, `Get-HealthResponse` port-forwards the observed pod and captures the body with GNU curl (`-D -` for the status line, `--retry-connrefused` for forward readiness), which never discards HTTP-error bodies; the fallback transcript is folded into the packet under a `port-forward fallback:` marker so both capture paths stay auditable, and the disclosed trade-off — this path traverses the API server, not the pod's own loopback, so it proves the endpoint's response rather than the image-native client path — is recorded in the function's own comment. Pinned by the new `test_health_probe_records_fallback_markers_and_captures_body_via_port_forward`, which also binds the ordering (in-container probe first, port-forward only after). Lanes re-run green: `production_deployment_evidence` 34/0/0, `publish_containers` 38/0/0, `CiTestInventoryTests` 62/0/0/0/0 on a fresh Release build, and the edited verifier parses clean under pwsh.

#### Accepted blocker - AC4 independent security approver (recorded 2026-07-26, code review chunk 3b)

- **Blocker:** AC4 requires Hexalith Platform Operations approval and a *separate* security reviewer approval of identity, secrets, TLS, network, authorization, encryption, privacy and evidence integrity. No independent security approver is assigned. The only assignment on record named the Platform Operations owner plus a persona acting for that same human, which does not satisfy the separation requirement and has been withdrawn.
- **Owner:** Product Owner, to nominate an independent security approver; Hexalith Platform Operations remains the AC4 operations approver.
- **Consequence:** C1 gates C1.23 (Platform Operations approval), C1.24 (non-HA acknowledgement) and C1.25 (security reviewer approval) cannot leave `pending`/`not complete`. C1 therefore cannot pass, Production lifecycle writes stay disabled, Story 27.3 stays `in-progress`, Story 27.4 stays `backlog`, and A41 stays open. This blocker is independent of, and additional to, the five external C1 inputs and the `.example.invalid` clock authorities.
- **Reopen trigger:** a named human other than the Platform Operations owner is recorded as the accountable security reviewer for Story 27.3, and records their AC4 approval against the immutable evidence packet.
- **Not a substitute:** no persona, agent, workflow, or self-review may be recorded as the independent security approver.
