- 2026-07-26 (third dev-story invocation): Applied all 38 open chunk-3a `[Review][Patch]` items and the patch work behind the 7 chunk-3a `[Review][Decision]` items, then re-derived C1 as still rejected. Task 1 remains unchecked and Story 27.3 remains `in-progress`; no Production enablement, live deployment mutation (`kubectl apply` was not run), approval claim, Story 27.4 artifact, or A41 mutation occurred. The 3 `[Review][Action]` items stay open: they are `correct-course` activities (`DW 27.3-CR14`/`CR15`/`CR16`), not dev-story scope.
  - **The one genuine product defect: `StaleIndex` orphaned the record it was protecting.** `DeleteAndVerifyAsync` decided bucket membership on `RecordId` alone, so a superseded or foreign expiry entry sharing `(ExpiryMinute, Shard)` with the live record pruned the *live* entry before returning `StaleIndex`. `GetDueEntriesAsync` reads purge candidates only from buckets, so that record could never be rediscovered — permanently unpurgeable, while the caller saw a clean denial. The fix matches the complete five-member entry identity in the membership check, in `CreateBucketRemoval`, in `RemoveBucketEntryAsync`, and in the post-delete verification re-read, and is mirrored in `InMemoryAccessTelemetryStateStore`, whose entry key now carries the full identity. Driven by a genuine RED: both tests that asserted the orphaning as correct failed on the fix; both now assert the opposite and additionally require the record to still be returned by `GetDueEntriesAsync`.
  - **The in-memory adapter no longer diverges from the adapter it stands in for.** All four behaviours the portable lifecycle checkpoints can observe are aligned: a duplicate entry slot returns `Conflict` decided before any mutation (it used to throw `ArgumentException` *after* `_records` was written — a non-atomic write); `VerificationFailed` is reachable through a `SuppressRecordDeletion` hook plus the same strong post-delete verification; `ttlInSeconds` is validated and observable instead of silently discarded; and the due scan is minute-major over an active-minute catalog that prunes drained minutes and leaves untraversed minutes alone. Seven new cases pin each behaviour. `LastTransactionOperationCount` is now derived from the committed operation set (3 opening a new minute, 2 otherwise — exactly the Dapr adapter's counts) and read under the lock, replacing a `ShouldBe(2)` tautology in two checkpoints.
  - **Guards that could not fail are now bound.** The three deployment-guard helpers were structurally unsound in five distinct ways; each is fixed and mutation-proven, including the one that mattered most — pg_hba is first-match-wins, so a bare `host … scram-sha-256` line above the reject rules accepted plaintext TCP while all four presence assertions passed. The ordering-parity tests never exercised the `Shard` or `RecordId` tiebreakers they claimed to pin; two fixtures now do, and removing either tiebreaker fails exactly one of them. The `TransactionalDaprState` fake now enforces the partition/TTL metadata contract and strong-only reads, so an adapter that stopped sending either no longer stays green.
  - **A manifest defect the connection-pool guard was blind to.** The guard hardcoded two writers while the shipped overlay is `replicas: 0` with no `strategy:` block, so Kubernetes' default 25% surge would have added a third sidecar pool (3 x 40 + 13 = 133 against `max_connections=100`) mid-probe. The Deployment now pins `maxSurge: 0`/`maxUnavailable: 1`, and the guard derives peak pods from the rendered overlay instead of a constant.
  - **The C1 verifier now has real producers and an honest packet.** `_run_dapr_version` and `calculate_capacity` were defined and called from no evidence path, so AC1's runtime identity was never captured and gate C1.13 had no producer at all; both are wired. Capacity arithmetic gained the missing `schedulerBytes` and host-filesystem-headroom operands, the `max(137438953472, ceil(base/4))` reclamation floor, a pinned durability multiplier, and admission against the approved 70/80/90% table. `_normalize_bytes` now rejects every unit-less value, so `"400"` meaning 400 GB can no longer read as 400 bytes. Command failures become recorded observations instead of tracebacks that leave no packet. The packet carries per-command timestamps, reviewed-source hashes, pre/post workload identity, and the seven AC3/AC4 attestation fields — all defaulting to `unrecorded`, never to a synthesized claim.

**File List correction 2026-07-27 by code review: the preceding `dev-story` note is withdrawn and the File List returns to 48 paths.** That note added `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/sprint-change-proposal-2026-07-19-story-27-3-c1-production-adapter.md` to the File List on the premise that it was "already declared in `## File Scope`", and asserted that `## File Scope` parses to 50 unique entries, duplicates the ADR, declares a path this File List names as an exclusion, and that the two preceding rows' `identical (48 unique paths each)` claim did not hold at HEAD. **Every one of those statements is false**, and three review layers plus an independent re-derivation agree. `## File Scope` carries two labelled sub-lists, and `tools/check-story-file-scope.py:36-39` registers `read/verify only:` in `TERMINATING_LABELS`, so the gate-parsed allow-list is exactly the 48 entries under `Allowed files for this story:`; the tool prints 48 unique entries with no duplicate. The ADR appears once per sub-list, not twice in one. `sprint-change-proposal-2026-07-26-memories-ci-cd-alignment.md` appears only under `Read/verify only:`, which is the consistent encoding of a named exclusion, not a contradiction of one. Parsed at HEAD, allow-list 48 and File List 48 had an empty symmetric difference in both directions - the preceding rows were correct and this File List is restored to match them. The executable consequence of the withdrawn addition: `tools/check-story-file-scope.py --story-key 27-3-production-adapter-and-deployment-profile --changed-files-file <that one path>` reports it under `Out-of-scope files` and exits `1`. **Named exclusion recorded in its place (2026-07-27 Administrator decision, option 1):** that proposal is excluded from this File List with owner and reason - it is the Administrator-approved authority that narrowed Story 27.3 to C1 qualification, classified `Read/verify only` in `## File Scope` alongside the ADR and the 2026-07-26 CI/CD-alignment proposal, and owned by the `correct-course` activity that produced it. `git log --diff-filter=A` does attribute its creation to Story 27.3's own commit `f474db15`, which is why it is named here rather than left silent; creation provenance does not make an approved approval artifact story-mutable. **ADR dual declaration (2026-07-27 Administrator decision, option 3):** `docs/dev/adr-27.1-001-access-telemetry-lifecycle.md` is declared under both `Allowed files for this story:` and `Read/verify only:`. The gate parses the allow-list only, so the `Read/verify only` qualifier - "the immutable `PG-ONPREM-1` profile authority; edited only through an approved course correction" - is **advisory and unenforced**: nothing mechanically prevents a commit from editing the ADR. That is recorded here rather than repaired, because enforcement belongs with `DW 27.3-CR14`, the correct-course that will edit the ADR to carry the approved `maxConns: 40` and the recomputed `profile_sha256`, and can set the permission correctly in the same change. This phase changed exactly seven already-declared paths: this story record, `deferred-work.md`, `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/tests/27-3-adapter-profile-evidence.md`, two files under the `adapter-profile-runs/**` glob, `tests/tooling/access_telemetry_lifecycle/test_adapter_profile.py`, and `tools/verify_access_telemetry_lifecycle.py`.

Diagnosis preceded repair: seeding the two bootstrap Secrets alone is insufficient because `statestore`/`pubsub`/`conversation-openai` carry `auth.secretStore: secretstore`, so the Redis state store's init issues a live vault read against the absent OpenBao; and the fix was empirically de-risked before authoring — a local standalone daprd 1.18.1 loaded the exact `secretstores.hashicorp.vault` component against the unreachable production address with a generated throwaway CA (`Component loaded: secretstore`), proving vault Init never contacts the server, while the Server registers no vault-backed health check and the vault-routed components reference the identical Secret name/key pairs the lane already seeds under existing RBAC. Implementation: `tools/verify-production-deployment.ps1` applies the rendered production manifests **verbatim**, then — while the applications are scaled to zero, so no consumer pod ever loads the vault-typed component — merge-patches Components `secretstore` and `access-telemetry-secrets` to `secretstores.kubernetes` (cluster object preserves names and scopes) and writes the `secret-store-substitution.json` disclosure; `tools/validate-production-deployment-evidence.ps1` fails any packet whose disclosure is missing or incomplete, so the substitution cannot happen silently.
